My answer is still no.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time in your life when you felt unusually upbeat, excited, or hyper — not just happy, but almost to the point where it was out of character for you?\n\nPatient: I guess... well, before this whole slump started, there was a time. I wouldn't say hyper, but... I just felt really sure of myself. Maybe too sure. And then I'd just... snap at people over little things. Like when the district manager said something about my store layouts, I just... I had no patience for it. But I wouldn't call it excited. Just... different.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall if there was a time when you felt abnormally upbeat or elevated, where you needed less sleep, were more talkative than usual, or had so many ideas that it was hard to keep track?\n\nPatient: I don't know about being more talkative. I just... felt like I needed to keep busy, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if there was a time when you felt abnormally upbeat, elevated, or irritable — not just happy or excited, but noticeably different from your usual self — and if that lasted for several days in a row?\n\nPatient: I don't really know. I didn't really keep track of how long it went on.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall if there was a time when you felt abnormally upbeat, energetic, or irritable — so much so that it was clearly different from your usual self — and this lasted for at least four days in a row?\n\nPatient: I suppose it could have been four days. Like I said, I wasn't keeping track. It was just a period where I was busy. I don't know exactly how many days it was.\n\nDoctor: So, during that time when you felt more energetic and were doing a lot after work, was there a period of at least four days in a row where your mood was noticeably elevated or irritable — like you felt unusually good, overly confident, or easily annoyed — different enough that someone close to you might have noticed?\n\nPatient: Well...
